國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國就業及企業活動持續改善，期中選舉牽動財政與科技監管政策 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數由 54.5 升至 55.5，連續第 10 個
月維持於擴張區間；新訂單、生產及庫存表現改善，顯示需求與生產動
能仍具韌性。惟投入成本仍處高檔，且關稅政策、地緣政治及供應鏈風
險猶存，後續發展仍有待進一步觀察。 
⚫ 10 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數為 54.5，較上月 54.9 略降 0.4 個
百分點，但已連續 28 個月維持擴張，服務業景氣持續增長。新訂單與
商業活動雖趨緩 ， 就業續呈擴張 ， 未完成訂單創2022 年 7 月以來高點 ；
新出口訂單重返擴張區間，顯示內外需求仍具韌性，惟關稅、能源價格
及成本壓力仍為後續觀察重點。 
⚫ 10 月美國非農就業增加 16.5 萬人，高於上月修正後之 6.0 萬人，失業
率由 4.2%降至 4.1%，加以前兩個月就業人數合計上修 8.4 萬人，顯示
勞動市場較前期有所回溫。惟新增就業仍主要集中於專業及商業服務、
醫療保健及運輸倉儲等部分服務業，製造業增幅相對有限，反映勞動市
場雖維持韌性，但部分就業品質及企業用工需求仍存在壓力。 
⚫ 9 月美國職位空缺數由 719.0 萬個增至 752.3 萬個，職缺率由 4.3%升至
4.5%，企業徵才需求有所回升；聘僱人數亦同步增加，聘僱率由 3.3%
升至 3.4%。整體而言，企業用人需求改善、裁員規模下降，勞動市場仍
維持穩健，供需情勢持續朝平衡方向發展。 
⚫ 9 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差 1,050 億美元，較上月減少 0.7%。出口成
長 1.9%，高於進口增幅 1.2%，主因資本財及黃豆等農產品出口增加；
進口則受資本財及消費財需求帶動 。 累計1 至 9 月貿易逆差較去年同期
縮減 12.2%，顯示對外貿易失衡情況有所改善。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 2026 年美國期中選舉呈現反執政黨效應 ， 民主黨重掌國會主導權 。 截至
11 月 8 日開票結果，民主黨已確定取得參、眾兩院多數席次，其中參議
院獲 54 席、共和黨 44 席；眾議院則由民主黨取得 254 席、共和黨 162
席。州長席次方面，民主黨掌握28 席，共和黨 20 席，惟部分選區仍在
計票，最終席次尚待確認。民主黨在多個關鍵州選戰中獲勝，反映選民
對高物價、能源價格及中東局勢等議題的不滿情緒升高。選後國會由民

主黨主導，將對川普政府形成較大制衡，未來財政、稅制及貿易政策推
動難度提高；雖有助抑制財政赤字擴張，但預算協商及債務上限議題之
不確定性可能升高。 
⚫ 美國民主黨於期中選舉後取得國會主導權，人工智慧監管政策受到關
注。參議員桑德斯等人提出禁止人工超級智慧法案，主張限制先進 AI
研發、設立聯邦人工智慧部，並加強安全監管。該法案尚未通過，後續
須關注立法進展及對科技產業的影響。 
短評 
⚫ 美國就業市場回穩，製造業與服務業持續擴張，顯示經濟仍具韌性，惟
產業表現不均、企業成本壓力偏高，通膨風險尚未消除。另一方面，貿
易逆差雖略有改善，但關稅及供應鏈不確定性仍存，加上期中選舉改變
國會權力結構 ， 未來財政 、 貿易及AI 監管政策恐面臨更多協商與制衡 ，
後續須關注政策變化對企業投資、經濟成長及金融市場之影響。

元 
區 
通膨壓力持續增強 
經濟 
⚫ 歐元區 10 月 HICP 通膨上升 0.2 個百分點至 4%。 
⚫ 歐元區 9 月失業率上升 0.1 個百分點為 6.5%。 
⚫ 歐元區 9 月零售額月減 0.7%、第三季季減 0.5%。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 為打擊從中國湧入的低價電商品，歐盟自 11 月 1 日起正式落實對境外
包裹加收 2 歐元處理費的措施；此外，歐盟執委會於 11 月 4 日正式提
出研究制定 「地緣經濟威懾協定」 ，以限制並反制中國對歐盟的貿易威
脅。 
⚫ 中國商務部於 11 月 6 日宣布，對原產於歐盟的進口「乙叉降冰片烯」
和「對位芳綸長絲」發起反傾銷立案調查，中歐貿易戰火持續延燒。 
短評 
⚫ 預期年底前布蘭特原油位於每桶 90 美元以上 ， 將對通膨帶來額外壓力 ，
並持續影響零售額表現。後續須關注能源漲價可能會透過運輸、物流等
成本管道傳導至核心商品。

通膨漲幅收斂，尚無輸入性通膨疑慮 
經濟 ⚫ 10 月消費者物價指數（CPI）年增 2.33%。 
短評 
⚫ 10 月通膨雖仍高於 2%，但漲勢已有收斂，物價大致維持溫和上漲；外
食費漲幅持續逾 3%，民生物價壓力仍較有感。另，進口物價雖大幅攀
升，但目前尚未明顯傳導至國內消費端，輸入性通膨壓力有限。展望未
來，11 月 CPI 漲幅可能略為回升，惟整體通膨走勢仍屬可控。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.11.9 
一、 美國—就業及企業活動持續改善，期中選舉牽動財政與科技監管政策 
2026 年 10 月美國非農就業人數增加 16.5 萬人，明顯高於上月修正後之 6.0
萬人，亦高於過去 12 個月平均每月增加 4.8 萬人之水準，企業聘僱動能有所回
升；失業率則由4.2%降至 4.1%，持續維持於 3 月以來 4.1%至 4.3%之區間，勞動
市場整體仍具穩定性。產業別方面，新增就業主要由專業及商業服務業增加 7.0
萬人、醫療保健業增加 4.2 萬人及運輸倉儲業增加 2.5 萬人所帶動，製造業亦增
加 1.9 萬人，其餘產業就業變動幅度相對有限。薪資方面，私人部門平均時薪升
至 38.0 美元，月增 0.4%、年增 3.1%，增幅較前月略有加快；平均每週工時則維
持 34.4 小時不變。此外，8 月及 9 月新增就業人數合計上修 8.4 萬人，反映上兩
月勞動市場表現較初估時有所改善。整體而言，10 月美國新增就業明顯回升、失
業率小幅下降，加上前兩個月就業數據獲得上修，勞動市場有所改善，整體仍具
韌性；惟新增就業主要集中於部分服務業，且因經濟因素從事兼職工作的人數增
加，反映勞動市場仍存在就業不足及產業表現不均的情況。 
2026 年 9 月美國職位空缺數(Job Openings) 由上月修正後之 719.0 萬個增加
33.3 萬個至 752.3 萬個，職缺率由4.3%升至 4.5%，企業徵才需求略有回溫；聘僱
人數由 527.1 萬人增加至 540.2 萬人，聘僱率由 3.3%升至 3.4%。總離職人數由
517.5 萬人增加至 531.9 萬人，離職率維持於 3.3%。就離職結構觀察，主動離職
(Quits)人數由 304.4 萬人增加至 336.5 萬人，主動離職率由 1.9%升至 2.1%，勞工
轉職意願有所回升；裁員與解僱(Layoffs and Discharges)人數則由 176.8 萬人減少
至 164.1 萬人，裁員率由1.1%降至 1.0%，企業人力調整壓力仍屬溫和。產業別方
面，職位空缺增加主要來自州及地方政府 (不含教育)、專業與商業服務業、製造
業及營建業；聘僱人數則以住宿與餐飲服務業增幅較為明顯；醫療保健與社會救
助業裁員人數則有所下降。整體而言，9 月美國職缺數與聘僱人數同步增加，主
動離職意願回升且裁員規模下降，顯示企業用人需求有所改善，勞動市場仍維持
穩健，惟供需關係持續朝較為平衡方向發展。 
2026 年 10 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI) 由上月的
54.5 升至 55.5，增加 1.0 點，連續第 10 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，製造業景

氣持續擴張。細項觀察，新訂單指數由55.3 升至 55.5，接單情況持續改善；生產
指數由 56.7 攀升至 59.0， 製造活動進一步增溫 ； 就業指數雖由52.7 微降至 52.4，
但仍維持擴張。供應商交貨指數由 59.0 微升至 59.1，反映交貨時間持續延長；庫
存指數由 48.6 升至 51.4，顯示企業備貨意願回升。此外，價格指數由 77.9 降至
75.2， 投入成本壓力雖略有緩和但仍處高檔 ； 未完成訂單指數雖由56.4 降至 54.6，
但仍在擴張區間。另一方面，新出口訂單指數由50.9 升至 51.2，進口指數由 51.0
升至 52.9，均維持擴張區間，顯示外需及企業採購需求仍具支撐。整體而言，10
月美國製造業活動持續穩健擴張，新訂單、生產及庫存表現改善，顯示需求與生
產動能仍具韌性；惟投入成本仍處相對高檔，加上關稅政策、地緣政治情勢及供
應鏈變數猶存，後續對企業成本控制及製造業景氣發展之影響仍值得持續關注。 
2026 年 10 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI)為 54.5，較上月
54.9 下降 0.4 個百分點，惟已連續第 28 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近
12 個月平均值 54.3，美國服務業景氣持續擴張。細項觀察，商業活動指數由56.5
降至 53.8，新訂單指數由 59.8 降至 55.9，顯示需求與營運動能較前月放緩，但仍
維持擴張；就業指數由 50.1 升至 51.4，連續第 2 個月處於擴張區間，反映企業用
人需求仍具韌性。供應商交貨指數由 53.2 升至 56.7，交貨時間進一步延長；價格
指數由 74.0 降至 73.0，雖較前月回落，但已連續 23 個月高於 60，企業成本壓力
仍高。庫存指數由 57.8 降至 55.4，未完成訂單指數則由 56.6 升至 58.0，創 2022
年 7 月以來最高，顯示需求累積情形持續增加。此外，新出口訂單指數由46.9 大
幅升至 55.2，重返擴張區間，進口指數亦由52.9 升至 54.2，顯示外部需求及採購
活動回溫。ISM 指出，關稅及燃料成本仍是企業最關注的供應鏈議題，許多企業
尚未完全將增加成本轉嫁給客戶，未來仍須持續關注關稅、能源價格及成本壓力
對服務業景氣與通膨之影響；惟值得注意的是，10 月所有主要指標均位於擴張區
間，為 2026 年 2 月以來首見，顯示整體服務業景氣基礎仍具支撐。 
2026 年 9 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差為 1,050 億美元，較上月減少 7 億美元
(-0.7%)。當月出口 3,215 億美元，較上月增加58 億美元(+1.9%)；進口 4,266 億美
元，增加 52 億美元(+1.2%)，出口增幅高於進口，使貿易逆差略為改善。其中，
商品貿易逆差縮減至 1,359 億美元(-0.5%)，服務貿易順差略降至 309 億美元(-
0.1%)。出口成長主要受資本財出口增加 38 億美元帶動，其中電腦、電腦周邊設
備及民用航空器表現較佳 ； 食品、 飼料及飲料出口亦增加19 億美元 ， 以黃豆增幅

最為顯著。進口方面，資本財進口增加116 億美元，消費財進口增加24 億美元，
惟工業用品及原材料進口減少 37 億美元。累計 2026 年 1 至 9 月，美國商品及服
務貿易逆差為 6,623 億美元，較 2025 年同期減少-12.2%；同期出口成長 11.6%，
進口增加 6.2%，顯示對外貿易失衡情況較去年同期有所改善。 
2026 年美國期中選舉呈現反執政黨效應 ， 民主黨重掌國會主導權 ， 截至2026
年 11 月 8 日，美國期中選舉開票結果顯示，民主黨已確定取得國會參、眾兩院
控制權。其中，參議院民主黨陣營取得 54 席，共和黨 44 席；眾議院民主黨取得
254 席，共和黨 162 席。此外，全美州長席次分布方面，民主黨掌握28 席，共和
黨 20 席 。由於部分選區仍在計票或尚未確認選舉結果 ， 最終席次仍待確定 。 民主
黨並於全國眾議院普選票(Popular Vote) 獲得較高得票率，且在德州、堪薩斯州、
北卡羅來納州、俄亥俄州及南卡羅來納州等關鍵州選戰取得突破，反映選民對高
物價、能源價格上漲、中東衝突及部分政策方向的不滿情緒升高。此次選舉終結
共和黨自 2024 年總統大選以來同時掌控白宮與國會兩院的局面，美國再度形成
「分裂政府」(Divided Government)格局。未來川普政府推動重要法案、預算案、
人事任命及稅制改革等政策，將面臨更大的國會制衡與協商壓力，民主黨亦可能
加強對行政部門的監督與調查。就經濟層面而言，市場預期大規模減稅、財政擴
張及部分貿易政策推動速度可能放緩，有助於降低財政赤字進一步擴大的風險；
惟兩黨對立加深 ， 亦可能提高預算協商 、 債務上限及政府融資等議題的不確定性 。 
美國民主黨於 2026 年 11 月期中選舉取得國會參、眾兩院控制權後，未來人
工智慧監管政策走向受到關注。其中，由無黨籍參議員桑德斯（ Bernie Sanders ）
及民主黨眾議員卡薩爾（Greg Casar ）於 9 月分別提出的《2026 年禁止人工超級
智慧法案》 （Ban Artificial Superintelligence Act of 2026） ，主張禁止開發、部署、
持有及資助具備超越人類認知能力或可能威脅人類安全的人工超級智慧 （ASI），
並設立內閣層級的聯邦「人工智慧部」（Department of Artif icial Intelligence），
統籌先進 AI 系統的安全審查及監管。此外，法案規劃在監管機構完成籌設並建
立相關安全規範前 ， 暫停訓練運算量達10²⁵次以上的先進 AI 系統進行訓練 、 修改
及微調，並限制未發布系統的部署。截至 11 月 8 日，該法案仍處於國會委員會
審議階段，尚未正式立法。隨著民主黨將於 2027 年 1 月取得國會主導權，後續
法案審議進展及其對美國 AI 技術發展、企業投資與國際競爭的影響，值得持續
關注。

7

二、歐元區—通膨壓力持續增強 
 歐元區 10 月 HICP 通膨上升 0.2 個百分點至 4%， 核心通膨(不含食品及能源)
則持平為 2.5%。該月食品及能源通膨皆持續上揚，反映國際原油價格居高不下，
並透過肥料、物流成本上漲，間接推升食品價格。另外，服務通膨上升 0.1 個百
分點至 3.3%， 部分反映交通費用上漲的影響 ， 惟服務通膨仍是低於今年上半年的
平均水準。展望未來，近期布蘭特原油雖回落至 100 美元內，但美伊談判陷入僵
局 ， 仍預期年底前布蘭特原油位於每桶90 美元以上 ， 將對通膨帶來額外壓力， 後
續須關注能源漲價可能會透過運輸、物流等成本管道傳導至核心商品及服務價格，
進而提高核心通膨黏性。 
 其他經濟數據包含：(1)歐元區 9 月失業率上升 0.1 個百分點為 6.5%，失業人
數增加 12.2 萬人，主要是法國失業人數增加較多所致，係因大規模抗議活動的影
響；(2)歐元區 9 月零售額月減 0.7%、第三季季減 0.5%，物價上漲帶來的負面影
響逐步顯現，預期第四季零售額將延續此一疲態。 
三、日本—薪資成長動能增強，春鬥效應持續擴散 
 9 月薪資年增 4.6%，創 1997 年以來最大增幅，除受夏季獎金大增 32.6%支撐
外，經常性薪資亦年增 3.7%， 顯示今年春鬥的加薪成果正逐步由大型企業擴散至
中小企業。隨企業獲利保持強勁，加上日本最大工會聯合組織 （Rengo） 已表態明
年將爭取更高加薪幅度，預期薪資成長動能仍可延續，有助支撐民間消費，但同
時也可能推升服務價格，增加通膨持續性。 
四、中國—關注 11 中旬川習會發展 
10 月官方製造業 PMI 下降 0.8 點至 49.3，主因十一長假前需求提前釋放，以
及外部環境不確定性升高所致。新訂單、生產指數分別降至 49.4、49.8，新出口
訂單亦降至 48.7。此外，生產及出廠價格指數分別降至56.8 及 51.7，反映成本與
售價漲幅已有收斂跡象；惟就業指數則持平於 48.4，顯示就業仍顯疲弱。不過，
RatingDog 製造業 PMI 略增 0.1 點至 52.1，反映 RatingDog 調查以中小型出口企
業為主，在全球 AI 相關需求的支持下，仍能保持穩定的擴張表現；惟RatingDog
製造業產出物價指數連續兩個月下調，投入物價指數則持續上揚，反映競爭環境
激烈，迫使廠商犧牲利潤以降價求售。

官方非製造業 PMI 下降 0.5 點至 49.7， 其中建築業商務活動指數由50.3 降至
48.0，服務業商務活動指數則小幅下降至 50，顯示十一長假消費動能並不特別活
躍 ； 非製造業就業指數雖微升至46.0， 但仍遠低於榮枯線 ， 不利於後續內需復甦 。
另外，RatingDog 服務業指數下降 0.8 點至 50.8，亦反映需求增速減弱，使得
RatingDog 綜合產出指數為 52，較 9 月的 52.4 回落，為近三個月最慢，製造業產
出增快為能抵銷服務業擴張放緩。 
展望未來 ， 中國製造業仍面臨外需不確定 、 內需復甦偏弱及價格競爭激烈等 ，
惟全球 AI 產業需求可望擴張，加上人行上調外匯存款準備率，有助緩和人民幣
升值對出口競爭力的壓力 ， 接有助於繼續支持出口需求 。 此外 ， 若11 月 APEC 期
間川習會釋出改善中美經貿關係訊號，將有助提振企業信心及出口前景，惟後續
仍須觀察全球需求及地緣政治發展。 
五、印度及東南亞—馬來西亞央行按兵不動；印尼、越南出口亮眼；泰國通膨壓
力上升 
(1) 印尼：9 月出口額達 275.9 億美元，創四年新高、年增 11.87%，非油氣出口較
去年成長 12.39%，以動植物油脂成長 89.04%為最，礦物燃料及電氣機械及設
備分別成長 12%、24.37%，抵銷石油和天然氣出口下滑 1%的影響，對美國、
中國出口分別成長 23.21%、11.82%。9 月進口額達 242.3 億美元、年增 18.41%，
增幅創 6 個月新低，其中成品油進口成長 37%，非石油和天然氣進口額年增
17.59%，以電氣機械設備(25.9%)及塑膠製品(16.3%)增幅較大，自中國進口額
大增 30.23%、自東協和歐盟進口分別成長 20.98%、18.21%。總計 9 月，貿易
順差為 33.6 億美元，2026 年前 9 個月，貿易順差 106.1 億美元，出口和進口
分別成長 5.58%和 19.67%。 
(2) 泰國：10 月消費者物價年增率(CPI)從 9 月的 2.82%飆升至 3.9%，3 年多來首
次突破泰國央行 1%-3%的目標區間，主要受油價上漲和洪水導致食品價格上
漲的影響，泰國商務部預計 2026 年整體通膨率平均在 1.8%-2.2%之間，但由
於基期較低，物價壓力可能在年底前持續走高。雖然通膨壓力顯著增強，但官
方評估此波物價飆漲應屬暫時現象。 
(3) 馬來西亞：馬來西亞央行(BNM)11 月會議將隔夜政策利率 （OPR） 維持在2.75%
不變，為連續第八次維持利率不變，符合市場預期。政策聲明強化對經濟成長

的樂觀評估，表示在強勁的出口表現和持續的內需支撐下，2026 年全年經濟
成長率有望突破 5%，預計 2027 年經濟活動在科技出口帶動下將保持韌性。
通膨方面 ， 央行指出地緣政治發展 、 大宗商品價格上漲及不利天氣狀況將對通
膨構成上行壓力，但現有的燃油和公用事業補貼應有助於抑制通膨 。整體而言 ，
央行目前政策立場仍支持物價穩定且可持續的經濟成長， 並樂觀看待未來經濟
及通膨前景的發展。 
(4) 越南: 10 月出口額年增 51.4%，達 646.7 億美元的歷史新高，其中電子產品出
口年增 102.6%為主要成長動力，機械設備出口年增 49.8%居次。同時，10 月
進口額達 573.6 億美元、年增幅略放緩至 43.1%，其中，電子產品進口成長
84.5%，能源進口則因油價上揚而大幅增加 74.1%。總計 10 月，貿易順差為
73.1 億美元，連續第二個月轉為順差；2026 年前 10 月，貿易逆差小幅收斂至
120.7 億美元，出口額成長放緩至 27.5%、進口額成長率為 37.4%。10 月 CPI
年通膨率小幅上升至 5.09%，創 5 月以來最高，其中，以交通運輸費用上漲
15.57%為最，酒精飲料和菸草 （4.40%） 、住房和公用事業 （6.50%） 及醫療保
健（1.75%）價格漲幅均有所加快，由於通膨已連三月突破央行目標區間，將
加大升息的壓力。 
六、台灣—通膨漲幅收斂，尚無輸入性通膨疑慮 
10 月消費者物價指數 （CPI） 年增2.33%，較 9 月的 2.74%明顯回落，顯示整
體通膨壓力已有降溫跡象，但仍近半年皆高於 2%。10 月物價漲幅收斂，主要受
蔬菜價格轉跌、電價漲幅縮小，以及部分商品與服務價格漲勢趨緩等因素影響 ，
惟外食費年增 3.01%， 已連續5 個月超過 3%， 民眾對物價上漲的感受仍較明顯 。
另，10 月以新台幣計價的進口物價年增 33.32%，創近 50 年最大漲幅，主要受國
際原油及 AI 需求推升電子零組件價格所致，惟相關電子零組件多供出口使用，
對國內消費端影響有限，加以政府採取油價平穩措施，因此主計總處認為目前並
無輸入性通膨問題 。展望未來，隨中秋節比較基期因素消退 ，主計總處預估11 月
CPI 年增率仍將高於 2%，且漲幅可能較 10 月擴大。

75x is between company’s avg. P/E at 62x and avg. +1 P/E at 91x. Maintain Buy.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - LandMark 
Price Target Risks and Methodology - LandMark Optoelectronics Corp.  
Valuation: We employ a discounted P/E methodology, applying a 61.5x target P/E 
multiple on our 2029E EPS, then discount it back to 2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, 
risk free rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), to derive our 12-month target 
price of NT$4,490. Our target P/E multiple is based on the same PEG&M ratio as peers in 
the photonics supply chain. We are Buy-rated on LandMark. 
Key risk: Slower-than-expected SiPh penetration; stronger-than-expected competition; 
Integrated Device Manufacturers (IDMs) offering Epitaxial (EPI) wafers in-house.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
(-) PC OEMs and ODMs: Intel (covered by Joseph Moore) expects 2H26 PC units to
fall 20%+ y/y as OEMs aggressively cut inventories amid weaker sell-through; the
correction has accelerated over the past two months. This aligns with Morgan

Stanley's estimate at a ~25% y/y decline  (report link).
(+) Server OEMs and ODMs: Intel guides for server CPU unit growth of 30%+ in
both 2026 and 2027, with upside if wafer/substrate supply improves. It sees server
CPU TAM rising from US$40bn today to >US$200bn in five years, driven mainly by
AI agents.
(+) ABF substrates:  Most of the previously discussed ~40% capacity increase is due
in 2H27; Intel expects key component purchase commitments to rise sharply over

the next several quarters. Intel is also prioritizing securing substrate capacity and
improving yields for the EMIB-T. Complex designs add yield risk, which could lower
effective output, worsening ABF substrate shortages but adding further support for
pricing.

Key Takeaways
We view negative PC comments as more bearish for OEMs and ODMs positioned
in the lower end consumer segment, like Acer and Compal.
Strong CPU unit growth would be positive for Wiwynn, Lenovo, and Lotes.
Tighter ABF substrate supply and stronger pricing would be positive for
Unimicron and NYPCB's revenue and margins.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Broader recovery supports record earnings. 3Q revenue was NT$597.3bn (+1% Q/
Q, +82% Y/Y), with gross margin of 3.6% (+0.2ppt Q/Q, -0.1ppt Y/Y, 0.3ppt above
MSe), and net profit of NT$10.3bn (+6% Q/Q, +170% Y/Y, 42% above MSe). Data-
center revenue fell 5% Q/Q on shipment timing, while industrial, automotive and
Future Electronics revenue increased sequentially. The richer mix lifted gross margin
19bp Q/Q to 3.60%; operating margin rose 18bp to 2.38%, with operating expenses

increasing only 2% Q/Q. Net financing costs rose 22% Q/Q to NT$1.94bn.
AI drives a stronger 4Q. Management guides 4Q revenue of NT$730-770bn, with
the NT$750bn midpoint up 26% Q/Q, as data-center and communications growth
outweigh seasonal declines across consumer applications, industrial and automotive.
The guided gross-margin midpoint falls 36bp Q/Q to 3.24% on mix, but operating
profit rises 19% Q/Q to NT$16.96bn as expenses grow only 1%. 4Q EPS guidance is

NT$8.07–9.05, with the NT$8.56 midpoint up 7% Q/Q. Financing costs of NT$2.6–
2.8bn and the larger weighted-average share base temper the translation from
operating-profit growth into EPS.
Growth broadens, but cash conversion matters. Future Electronics grew over 40%
Y/Y in 3Q and has started shipping data-center products. Management remains
positive on 2027 AI demand, without providing a full-year earnings forecast.
Inventory days increased Q/Q from 47 to 66  and net debt from NT$27.6bn to NT

$46.4bn, although longer payables kept the cash-conversion cycle near 19 days
versus 18 in 2Q. We see further earnings potential from AI scale and Future’s
recovery, with funding requirements and inventory conversion the key execution
risks.
What’s Changed
WT Microelectronics Co. Ltd. (3036.TW) From To
Price Target NT$349.00 NT$296.00
WT Microelectronics Co. Ltd.  | Asia Pacific
AI shipments reaccelerate, with
funding costs rising
WT expects a sharp 4Q revenue rebound as datacenter

shipments accelerate, supporting further profit growth despite a
less favorable mix. The key earnings sensitivity is how much
operating leverage offsets rising funding costs.

4
Valuation methodology
We cut our price target by 15% to  NT$296: We update our residual income model to
reflect earnings estimate revisions. We increase our equity risk premium assumption from
11.8% to 12.5% to reflect segment volatilities, and reduce our intermediate growth rate
assumption from 10% to 8% to reflect a more conservative medium-term growth forecast.
We keep other key RI assumptions unchanged: 2.0% risk-free rate, 0.9 beta, cash dividend

payout ratio of 85%, and a long-term growth rate of 3.0%. Bull case PT is cut from NT
$406 to NT$344.5, implying 10.6x 2027 P/E. Bear case PT is cut from NT$230 to NT$160,
implying 4.9x 2027 P/E. The risk reward is now skewed more towards the bear case due to
potential downstream demand disruption.
Exhibit 3: WT: Residual Income Model
Source: Morgan Stanley Research estimates

continued progress in penetrating the AI supply chain.

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

文曄(3036) 
26Q4資料中心恢復成長  
投資評等 目標價 
逢低買進 250 
前次投資建議 2026.8.6： 
「逢低買進」，目標價：$300

報告日期：2026.11.6 研 究 員：林秀泓

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 26Q4 資料中心產品恢復強勁季增，通訊、資料中心產品受益 AI需求。 
➢ 2026 營收翻倍，2027營收增速減緩。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/5 收盤價： 195.00 ➢ 26Q3 營收 5,973 億元，QoQ+1.1%，YoY+81.6%，符合
財測 QoQ-3.2%~+3.6%。毛利率 3.60%(QoQ+0.19pt，
YoY-0.14pt)，符合財測 3.5%~3.6%，主要因為產品組合
改善。EPS 7.97元。 
➢ 26Q4 文曄財測以美元匯率 32.2 估計，預估營收
7,300~7,770 億元(QoQ+22.2%~+30.1%)，主要由資料中
心出貨恢復帶動。毛利率 3.2%~3.3%。營業費用
7,154~7,546 億元。財測 EPS 8.07~9.05 元。 
➢ 展望 26Q4 各應用別(以美元估計)，汽車電子受提前拉貨
影響，預期季減雙位數百分比；工業與儀器因季節性因
素，預期季減中個位數百分比；通訊受益於 AI 光通訊需
求，預期季增高雙位數百分比；資料中心與伺服器預期季
增高雙位數百分比，全年成長約兩倍；消費性產品因提前
拉貨，預期季減中雙位數百分比；PC 預期季減；手機旺
季提前拉貨後，預期季減低雙位數百分比。 
➢ 調整預估 2026/2027 年營收 24,151 億元/27,170 億元，
YoY+105.0%/+12.5%，EPS 28.85 元/33.57 元。 
➢ 目前股價處於 6X 27(F) PER，為過去 PE區間 7~40X的
低位。考量 2026 年 AI 帶動營收大幅成長，2027 年 YoY
增速明顯減緩，投資評等維持「逢低買進」 ，調整目標價
250 元(7X 27(F) PER)，潛在上漲空間 28%。

營 運 分 析

文曄為 IC通路代理商 
文曄成立於 1993/12 成立，2002/08 上市掛牌，為提供全球專業電子零組件
通路服務的廠商，透過合併多家 IC通路商的方式持續擴大公司規模。營運總
部設立於台灣，目前在中國、韓國、新加坡、印度、泰國、馬來西亞、越南
等地區共有五十多個營運據點。代理半導體原廠超過 400 家，服務客戶超過
25,000 家。根據 2025 年 Gartner 最新數據，文曄已經成為全球最大和亞太
區最大的半導體通路商，市佔分別為 15.1%和 17.0%。 
產品組合方面，以代理產品的應用劃分營收，26Q3 汽車電子佔 5%、工業與
儀器佔 9%、通訊佔 13%、資料中心及伺服器佔 56%、消費性及其他佔 5%、
個人電腦與週邊佔 4%、手機佔 9%。

26Q3 財報符合財測，獲利再創單季新高 
26Q3 營收 5,973 億元，QoQ+1.1%，YoY+81.6%，符合財測 5,720~6,120
億元(QoQ-3.2%~+3.6%)。毛利率 3.60%(QoQ+0.19pt，YoY-0.14pt)，符合
財測 3.5%~3.6%，主要因為高毛利的工業、車用、Future 營收季增，產品組
合改善。營業費用QoQ+1.6%，費用率1.22%維持低檔，營業槓桿效益持續。
營業利益 142.3 億元，QoQ+9.5%，YoY+151.5%，優於財測中位數 140 億
元。業外方面，淨財務成本約19.4 億元，高於原先預估，因為資金成本上升。
其他業外收入 5.8 億元主要來自權益法認列日電貿的獲利，以及股利收入。
淨利 103.0 億元，QoQ+6.0%，YoY+169.9%，EPS 7.97元。

26Q4 營收預估季增，資料中心恢復成長 
26Q4 文曄財測以美元匯率 32.2 估計，預估營收 7,300~7,770 億元
(QoQ+22.2%~+30.1%，YoY+113.4%~+127.1%)，主要由資料中心出貨恢復
帶動 。 毛利率3.2%~3.3%。 營業費用7,154~7,546億元 。 財測EPS 8.07~9.05
元。

26Q4 資料中心恢復高雙位數季增，通訊持續成長 
26Q3 分產品線來看(以新台幣計)，汽車電子 QoQ+18%，優於前次法說預期
季減中低個位數百分比；工業與儀器 QoQ+13%，優於預期季增個位數百分
比；通訊 QoQ+5%，符合預期；資料中心與伺服器 QoQ-5%、YoY+114%，
符合預期；消費性產品 QoQ-7%，符合預期；PC週邊 QoQ持平，優於預期
季減個位數百分比，若不含記憶體則QoQ-11%、YoY-20%；手機QoQ+32%，
符合預期，受惠旺季與提前拉貨。 
展望 26Q4 各應用別(以美元估計)，汽車電子：受提前拉貨及庫存調整影響，
預期季減雙位數百分比，YoY+20%以上，全年YoY+30%以上；工業與儀器：
季節性因素，預期季減中個位數百分比，YoY+40%以上，全年成長約40%；
通訊：受益於 AI光通訊需求，預期季增高雙位數百分比，全年成長約一倍；
資料中心與伺服器：出貨節奏調整後恢復，預期季增高雙位數百分比，全年
成長約兩倍，2027 年預期維持高速成長；消費性產品：因提前拉貨，預期季
減中雙位數百分比；PC：預期季減；手機：旺季提前拉貨後，預期季減低雙
位數百分比。

半導體市場成長再度上修 
CSP 的 AI 資本支出展望正向，AI 需求強且具結構性。根據 Gartner 數據，
2026 年全球半導體市場由原先預估 YoY+92%再度上修到 YoY+108%，2027
年預估 YoY+39.2%(不含記憶體+33%)。半導體市場 2025~2029 年 CAGR
由 25.9%上修到 35.1%，2029 年市場規模預估達 2.7 兆美元。data center
為成長最強勁的領域，2026/2027 年預估 YoY+171%/+60%，2025~2029 年
CAGR+54.9%。 
文曄 26Q3 資料中心受出貨節奏影響，26Q4 恢復強勁季增，2026 全年成長
約兩倍，2027 年預期維持高速成長；通訊受益於 AI 光通訊需求，成長動能
延續。Future 方面，歐美市場持續正向復甦，26Q3 營收 YoY+40%以上，車
用與歐洲工廠自動化已無去庫存壓力，資料中心業務也開始接單出貨。Future
較高毛利率可部分抵銷 AI 產品線低毛利率的影響。調整預估 2026/2027 年
營收 24,151 億元/27,170 億元，YoY+105.0%/+12.5%，淨利 387 億元/450
億元，YoY+185.9%/+16.3%，EPS 28.85 元/33.57 元。

目前股價處於 6X 27(F) PER，為過去 PE區間 7~40X的低位。考量 2026 年
AI 帶動營收大幅成長，2027 年 YoY 增速明顯減緩，投資評等維持「逢低買
進」 ，調整目標價 250 元(7X 27(F) PER)，潛在上漲空間 28%。

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감한화에어로스페이스(012450) 방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실_16454052.pdf

한화에어로스페이스 (012450)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 방위산업 | 2026. 11. 2  
방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감

방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감 
지상방산 매출 1조 2,450억원으로 전년비 41%, 영업이익 217억원으로 전년비 
96.2% 급감하며 영업이익률은 1.7% 기록. 실적 급감에 대해 사측은 올해 주요 사업
의 납품 일정이 4분기에 집중됨에 따라 3분기 인도 물량이 적었던 것과 대전 사업장 
생산 중단에 따른 영향 때문이라고 설명. 단, 4분기에는 당초 계획에 따라 주요 사업의 
납품 물량이 집중되어 있어 실적이 개선될 것이고 대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 내에 정
상 가돌 하는 것이 목표라고 밝힘 
수주잔고 증가세 지속으로 성장 지속 가능할 것 
3분기 말 수주잔고 35조원으로 전년비 12.9% 증가. 이를 통한 지상방산 부문 성장 
지속 가능할 것으로 판단. 참고로 사측은 내년 매출 15% 수준 성장 전망 유지. 하지
만 금리 상승에 따른 글로벌 업종 평균 밸류에이션 하락에 따른 방산 부문 적용 멀티
플 하향 조정 그리고 상장 연결회사(한화오션, 한화시스템, 쎄트렉아이)와 상장 비연결
회사(한국항공우주)의 주가 하락 반영 영향으로 목표주가는 하향 조정 
8월 미 육군 차륜형 자주포 사업에서 시제 사업자로 선정. 향후 K9MH는 미 육군의 
작전 운용 개념에 맞춰 맞춤 조정을 거친 후에 특별한 결함 혹은 결격 사유가 없으면 
최종 계약 체결 예정. 동시에 미 육군의 궤도형 자주포 사업에도 참여할 계획. 뿐만 아
니라 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등으로 신시장 개척 역시 지속되고 있다는 점도 긍
정적 
 Analyst 이재광 
rheejaekwang @lssec.co.kr

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
We reaffirm our expectation  that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board will again increase the cash rate at the November
meeting. Today’s inflation data and Senate testimony are
the final pieces of information before the RBA Monetary
Policy Board (MPB) meets next week. Recent data and

communication have consistently pointed to the staff
recommending an increase in the cash rate. We expect a
majority of the external members to agree, though some
members might dissent.
Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions. It

will therefore deem that further policy tightening is needed.
The RBA has made it clear that it is down-weighting the recent
easing in the labour market, even though this was more than it
forecast. In today’s opening statement  to the Senate hearing,
the Governor said, “Despite the unemployment rate recently
rising a little more than we had expected, the labour market
seems a little tighter than we had previously judged. The
evidence for this is elevated underlying inflationary pressures.

That is, when we exclude some of the large volatile price
movements such as fuel we see inflationary pressure in areas
that reflect domestic demand for goods and services.” In the
Senate, the Governor also said that they were not worried
about the current pace of increase in unemployment.
Of course, this takes no account of the lagged relationship
between the labour market and inflation. The argument is that
if that measure of inflation last quarter was too high, it must

therefore be that the labour market is too tight. Nor does it
allow sufficiently for non-labour cost shocks, which business
surveys indicate are the real issue. But whether this judgement
by the RBA turns out to be right or not will not be revealed
for some time, certainly not soon enough to change its mind
before next week. And while some MPB members might have
a more optimistic view about labour supply trends than the
RBA staff, they are likely to be a minority.

The RBA is also relatively sanguine about the current
downturn in the housing market. The Governor and other
RBA officials are at pains to highlight that they do not target
housing prices; nor should they. The RBA considers that most,
though not all, of the current downswing is an expected effect
of tighter monetary policy.
RBA to hike in November, as inflation remains too high
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The question then turns to the future beyond November. While

we still expect a hawkish tone in the RBA’s communication
until inflation has clearly turned down, further hikes beyond
next week are not currently our base case. Clearly, though, the
risks of another hike – most likely in February – have increased.
Our current forecast for Q4 inflation would seem to give them
cover for such a move. We note, however, that home-building
costs in the CPI have undershot expectations in the past couple
of months. This might imply a different view of the extent of

domestic demand pressures than the RBA currently believes.
However, a lot can happen in three months, as we have just
seen. The conflict in the Middle East  could escalate further,
lifting energy prices beyond our current base case. It could
also go the other way, though, if the US and Iran manage to
strike a post-midterm-election deal. And as their counterparts
in New Zealand highlighted today, one risk is that the global
economy weakens suddenly. In the post-meeting statement ,

the RBNZ Monetary Policy Committee said, “the world
economy could tip abruptly into a downturn causing weaker
inflation over the medium term.” This risk was something the
RBNZ MPC thought could crystallise in the next six weeks.
Whether on this timetable or something more drawn out, this
possibility does not appear to be on the RBA’s radar. Nor is it
our base case, though we have previously worried that the AI
financing boom could hit a wall quite suddenly.

“We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA
Monetary Policy Board will again increase the
cash rate at the November meeting"

### Page 3

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related
risks through 2027; although, in the absence of another shock,
we expect them to remain on hold.
The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view

on the breadth of growth, the 3.4% annualised gain for GDP
benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household
consumption, 12.7% growth in non-residential investment
as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment
was effectively offset by net exports’ drag given most of
the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is
imported. This is not to say that US firms won’t benefit from

the investment once complete, but simply that much of the
initial return goes to the foreign businesses who provide the
necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks
remain for the consumer, the increase in nominal spending
seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis,
consumption flat in the month following a 0.5% gain in
August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the
outlook is expected to see household consumption growth

average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE
inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr medium-term target as
the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline
and core measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy
rates unchanged in October. Of note from the meeting
communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks

for inflation, but also downside risks for growth. "While the
full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying
inflation has remained broadly stable so far and wages do not
show a material response". "Financing conditions have [also]
tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth".
In the press conference, President Lagarde noted that,
between the October and December meetings, a significant
amount of new economic information will be released,

allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.
Based on the October meeting communications, we continue
to expect a rate hike at the December meeting but believe this
will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the
spectrum of risks Europe faces is broad and skewed towards
inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that
she will step down as ECB President in February ahead of the

scheduled conclusion of her term next October.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI .
Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in the month, largely
owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in
domestic and international holiday travel & accommodation.
The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than
anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).
While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation –
housing in particular looks to be cooling faster than expected

as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are
still showing persistence. Highlighting this is market services
ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator
of price pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this
metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail points
to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for
another chunky read in Q4.
With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts,

and some of the energy risks flagged then materialising, we
continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp
rate hike to 4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate
hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market
expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding instead to wait for
further information before taking the next step with policy. The
decision was made 4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A

25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening in 2027
(Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems
probable; however, each decision will depend on fuel prices,
inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady  at its October
meeting. Based on prior comments by key members, the
decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook

and Cleveland Fed President Hammack’s dissents in favour
of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at
a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Chair
Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic
growth outside of tech-related investment (evinced by Q3
GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in
the statement. However, inflation “remains elevated” and the

FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable
return of annual inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will
help the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests
another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter, the FOMC
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
A lesson to be learned
The RBNZ delivered the biggest shock to markets outside of
a crisis period in over 20 years this week by leaving the OCR
unchanged. Markets had priced around a 98% probability of
a 25bp increase, and all major domestic analysts concurred.

After the CPI data the week earlier, we belatedly shifted our
call to a hike despite plenty of good arguments in favour of
the RBNZ sticking to its plan to pause. With the benefit of
hindsight, too much weight was put on market pricing and
short-term inflation drivers and not enough on the medium
term. That’s an important lesson to be learned.
The highlight of an action-packed week of central banking news
was the unexpected decision of the RBNZ to leave the OCR at

2.75%, despite near unanimous analyst expectations and market
pricing in favour of a 25bp hike. We had shifted sides from a
hold to a hike just a week earlier, in the wake of a CPI outcome
that was weaker than RBNZ expectations on the surface, but
with some evidence of firm core inflation undertones.
While we clearly cannot claim to have gotten this call right,
we always thought that the outcome was not the “done deal”
that markets had priced in. For example, in our preview of the

meeting we noted:
“We still think a more measured tightening approach is
appropriate from here. Raising the OCR at every meeting
until something ‘breaks’ could be a risky strategy if downside
growth concerns crystalise.”
And we saw a vote on the decision as very possible:
“A vote seems very possible as differences in view will likely
remain among the MPC about how much the medium-term
inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.”
The 4:3 vote that was ultimately required to bring the MPC

to a decision is a testament to just how finely balanced the
decision was. We had thought that the hike was perhaps
around a 60-70% probability whereas the Record of Meeting
implies something more like a 50:50 call.
In the final analysis, the RBNZ has merely followed through on
the pause it signalled for this meeting back in the September
Monetary Policy Statement. All MPC members continue to see
a case for a higher OCR in time, but there are different views

around the timing of those increases. Governor Breman noted
that further interest rate increases remain likely in coming
meetings but are conditional on the whether the outlook
evolves in line with expectations. Indeed, the Governor noted
in later comments to press that a December hike was a “high”
probability and that the outlook remains for OCR “hikes” as the
base case. This suggests that the RBNZ’s view on the terminal
OCR hasn’t really shifted down from the 3.25% level presented

back in September. And it may be reasonable to speculate,
subject to data evolving as the RBNZ expects, that a modest
upgrade in those forward OCR forecasts could be in the offing.
So how did we all get this so wrong? This outcome was a real
shock given the historical record shows that over the history of
the OCR the market has without exception correctly predicted
a 25bp hike when the implied probability built into markets
was as high as it was. Clearly some recalibration of the model

is required. We can identify two key areas where lessons
should be learned: too much weight on market pricing; and not
enough on drivers of medium-term inflation.
Market pricing did not adequately reflect the
MPC’s desire to shift strategy .
Firstly, and most importantly, we put too much weight on
market pricing and too little on the RBNZ’s clearly stated
strategy of preferring a pause at the October meeting to
assess the impact of interest rate increases already done. In our

Preview we incorrectly surmised that silence equated to assent
and that market pricing of a hike would make an OCR hike the
less disruptive option – especially given the sense that a hike
to 3% was still likely in December as implied by the RBNZ’s
September forecasts. From a macroeconomic perspective,
raising rates six weeks early is of little consequence.
This logic, as sound as it was, was wrong, as market pricing did
not reflect a large enough change in the medium-term inflation

outlook to justify a change in RBNZ strategy. Markets at least
partially held the view, based on a long history of revealed
RBNZ preference, that the RBNZ keeps moving the OCR in the
same direction until confronted with compelling evidence that
a halt to the cycle is in prospect. That operating approach may
be under review now.
It’s not uncommon for other central banks to take breaks
while amid an interest rate cycle to gather information on
the impact of work already done before moving forward.

This week’s pause by the FOMC in the face of a fierce set of
monthly inflation readings in the last month is an example.
Pausing does not need to imply stopping or reversing. Indeed,
taking a more measured approach could prove to be solid
risk management – as we identified in our MPR preview note.
Risk management concerns probably should have greater
prominence in this cycle given we are seeing a measure of
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
supply side cost shocks that might not be persistent. The
Statement of Record contains a wealth of material related to
these sorts of uncertainties and concerns. Markets were not
listening to these concerns, however.
An insufficient focus on drivers of medium-term

inflation
Markets were not listening to the alarm bells because there
was a heavy focus on factors that may be more relevant to the
short-term inflation outlook than medium-term inflation. Top
of mind were the rise in energy prices and fall in the exchange
rate. Both factors look set to significantly boost the short-
term inflation outlook – for example we currently forecast
December quarter annual inflation of 4.3%, with upside risks
from there if fuel prices do not fall over the balance of the

quarter. However, our analysis of the data surprises suggested
no significant shift to the implied RBNZ OCR track one year
ahead. Upward pressure on the future OCR projection from
the lower exchange rate and stronger GDP was balanced
by higher long term interest rates and the lower Q3 CPI
outcome. Higher energy prices were not seen as a key factor
as they don’t impact medium term inflation unless inflation
expectations are dislodged (no evidence was found of that

since September) or if the increase in energy prices is viewed
as persistent (futures markets continue to imply lower forward
energy prices, although MPC hawks clearly see risks of
persistent inflation coming from supply shocks).
The RBNZ’s communications at the MPR and in its aftermath
are directing markets to pay more attention to medium-term
inflation drivers in the future. Hence the references to the
strength of demand and inflation expectations. The risk to

the RBNZ’s inflation forecasts are to the upside, and hence
we might reasonably expect some upgrade of the OCR profile
when we see new forecasts in December. But it’s not a done
deal by any means as data and events between now and then
will dictate what stance the RBNZ takes. This week’s business
and consumer confidence reports do not seem to present many
smoking guns of imminent second-round inflation pressures,
even though business and consumer sentiment seems to be

taking the higher oil price in its stride.
Is core inflation high enough yet to shift the
strategy?
Another interesting point made by the Governor was that the
markets (and analysts) misinterpreted how the September
quarter CPI was read by the RBNZ, which evidently took
greater comfort from the outcome. Governor Breman has noted
she wished the RBNZ has protocols in place to communicate
with markets the RBNZ’s view on that CPI data given that it
seems to have been a factor that figured importantly in at least

some MPC member’s thinking. Headline inflation was lower
than the RBNZ expected and importantly the core inflation
measures, that we had thought might have been of concern to
the MPC, do not yet seem to have been at a level sufficient to
shift the RBNZ from its pause-then-hike strategy. We remain
sceptical that a 3.25% OCR will prove sufficient to bring core
inflation down significantly. But core inflation appears to need
to lift for the MPC to become more agitated. It likely will lift,

but we won’t get more data on that until January 2027.
December hike likely but not a done deal
We expect a further 25bp hike at this year’s final meeting on 9
December and then 25bp increases in the February and March
2027 meetings, followed by a slower pace of increases at the
June and September Monetary Policy Statement meetings.
Our forecast of a peak OCR of 4% from September 2027 has
remained unchanged throughout, as has our assessment of a
3.75% neutral OCR.

That said, in the current uncertain environment, it goes without
saying that the evolution of monetary policy will depend on
how both global events and key data evolve. Therefore, as the
RBNZ noted itself, the exact timing of the tightening profile
is highly uncertain and even the tightening we forecast at the
December meeting should not be regarded as a done deal.
Looking ahead, the ‘hardest’ data to come will be the Q3
labour market surveys where we anticipate a small fall in

the unemployment rate to 5.5% but still moderate wage
pressures. The December quarter RBNZ inflation expectations
surveys will be of significant interest (17, 20 and 24
November) for MPC members looking for persistent inflation
impacts. The usual run of monthly activity and selected prices
indicators will be useful in narrowing down Q4 growth and
inflation momentum. And finally, the MPC’s focus on external
risks will imply keeping an eye on global fixed income, equity

and credit markets for signs of imminent calamity.
Finally, November 7 is the general election. A close race is
envisaged that could impact on the RBNZ’s mandate. We
will be offering client briefings on Monday 9 November and
Tuesday 10 November to outline the immediate implications
of the election outcome for markets and the economy. Contact
your relationship manager for details.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: RBA Policy Decision (%)
Nov 3, Last: 4.60, Westpac f/c: 4.85
Market f/c: 4.85, Range: 4.60 to 4.85
We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) will decide to raise the cash rate by 25bps to
4.85% at the November meeting.

Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions.
Recent data and communication have consistently pointed to
the RBA staff recommending an increase in the cash rate. We
expect a majority of the external board members to agree,

though some members might dissent.
AUS: Oct Cotality Home Value Index (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –1.1, Westpac f/c: –1.3
The Cotality home value index – covering all of Australia – fell
1.1% in Sep with downward revisions shaving a further 0.4%
off prices. The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2%
from their March peak, annual growth stalling flat. Cotality
reported 97% of capital city suburbs experienced falls over the
three months to September.
The October update is set to be another weak one with an

added hit coming from the RBA’s interest rate rise in late
September. The daily measure (covering the five major capital
cities rather than all of Australia) is tracking a 1.5% decline vs
1.3% in September. The detail points to slightly bigger falls
in Sydney, Brisbane and Perth with Melbourne continuing
to record more moderate declines. Note that seasonality is
usually a slight positive in the October month.
AUS: Sep Dwelling Approvals (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –6.1, Westpac f/c: –7.0

Market f/c: 2.0, Range: –7.0 to 5.0
Dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in August, with the annual
pace easing slightly to 10.3%yr. Weakness centred on unit
approvals with private detached house approvals actually
posting a solid rebound from a July month fall, touching a new
cycle high (up 18.4%yr).
We expect the September update to show a much clearer
weakening. New construction is coming under pressure
from multiple fronts: interest rate rises; a sharp downturn in

established markets; and rising costs – with a great deal of
uncertainty around each of these. New home sales have fell
sharply through the September quarter and we expect to
see detached house approvals show something similar. Unit
approvals are coming off a weak read and should be more
stable. Total approvals are expected to print 7% lower.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Goods Trade Balance ($bn)
Nov 5, Last: 0.5, Westpac f/c: 2.0
Market f/c: 1.0, Range: –1.0 to 2.5
Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August.
Recently, the trade balance has been oscillating between
small deficits and surpluses with significant volatility in the

underlying trade mix.
Swings in non-monetary gold, fuel and cars have all played
a large role, but the most eye-catching result from Aug was
the 79% surge in automatic data processing equipment (ADP)
imports, which captures the investment upswing related to
data centres.
For September, we expect an increase in the surplus as the
ADP import surge unwinds, though we are likely in store for
ongoing monthly volatility in trade for some time.
NZ: Sep Building Consents (%mth)

Nov 3, Last: +5.6%, Westpac f/c: Flat
Annual residential building consent numbers are currently at
a three year high, and we expect that they will remain firm in
the September update. However, monthly consent numbers
are showing signs of peaking, with falls in three of the last four
months. We’re watching to see if that trend has continued,
particularly given the ongoing softness in the housing
market.  In the non-residential space, we’ve seen softness

in the number of retail and office developments, but greater
resilience in the industrial and storage segment.
AUS: Sep Household Spending Indicator (%mth)
Nov 4, Last: flat, Westpac f/c: –0.5
Market f/c: 0.2, Range: –0.5 to 0.8
The household spending indicator was unchanged in August,
annual growth easing to 6.8%yr. The detail showed a modest
rotation away from discretionary categories towards non-
discretionary.
Our Westpac-DataX Card Tracker  showed a decent rise in

the September month although new vehicle sales (which are
not covered by the tracker but are in scope for the spending
indicator) look to have posted a decline. Overall we expect
the indicator to show a 0.5% decline in the September month.
Note that the update with also include Q3 estimates of real
spending – we expect this to show a 0.6%qtr gain with prices
up 1.2%qtr and nominal spend up 1.8%qtr.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 Labour Market Surveys (%)
Nov 4, Unemployment Rate, Last: 5.6, Westpac f/c: 5.5
LCI Private Sector, Last: 0.7, Westpac f/c: 0.5
We expect the unemployment rate to edge down to 5.5%
for the September quarter. Job numbers have been gaining
momentum in recent months, and now look to be enough to

outstrip population growth. However, the existing slack in the
labour market is likely to be keeping wage growth subdued,
despite cost-of-living pressures on workers. Our forecasts are
a little more positive overall than what the RBNZ assumed in
its September MPS forecasts.
US: Oct Employment Report
Nov 6, Payrolls, Last: 29k, Market f/c: 55k, WBC f/c: 75k
Nov 6, U/E rate, Last: 4.2%, Market f/c: 4.2%, WBC f/c: 4.3%
Nonfarm payrolls have shown considerable volatility of late,

July’s -10k followed by a 133k gain in August then a 29k
increase in September. However, the 3-month average of 51k
is likely a good guide on underlying momentum. In October, we
look for a 75k gain, although we expect some of that increase
to be offset by a modest reduction to the prior two months,
keeping the 3-month average around 50k.
While the participation rate has lifted in recent months, it
remains well below the average of 2025. Month-to-month, a

change either way is not guaranteed, but it seems likely that
the participation rate will trend higher in coming months. We
therefore expect the unemployment rate to edge up.

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, +68.24 điểm tương ứng +4.00%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Thiết bị điện, Khách sạn - Nhà hàng và Thiết bị dịch vụ y tế là các nhóm ngành có 
mức tăng rõ rệt nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần 
trước với giá trị -966 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VCB; mặt khác, tự doanh duy 
trì mua ròng ở mức 137 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,335 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -3,049 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng 
1,815 hợp đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,000 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam hồi phục trở lại sau bốn tuần giảm liên tiếp, khi VN-Index bật tăng mạnh 
từ vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1.700 điểm với thanh khoản cải thiện. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh kéo dài 
khiến mặt bằng định giá về vùng hấp dẫn, lực cầu bắt đáy đã nhập cuộc quyết liệt hơn, 
với phiên 21/10 tăng gần 47 điểm — một trong những phiên tăng mạnh nhất nhiều tháng 
gần đây. Điểm tích cực và đáng chú ý nhất là khối ngoại đã xuất hiện hai phiên đảo chiều 
mua ròng trở lại sau khoảng 20 phiên bán ròng liên tiếp, tập trung vào nhóm Bluechip 
như VIC, TCB, VHM, MSN — tín hiệu đầu tiên cho thấy áp lực rút vốn ngoại có thể đang dần 
hạ nhiệt. Thông tin tích cực tác động lên thị trường đến từ các chỉ đạo của NHNN và Chính 
phủ về việc hạ mặt bằng lãi suất để hỗ trợ tăng trưởng kinh tế. Bên cạnh đó, mùa báo cáo 
KQKD quý 3 khả quan đang diễn ra cũng là yếu tố hỗ trợ xu hướng thị trường chung.

19/10/2026

Nguồn: V_KBSource

Triển vọng kinh tế 4Q2026 
Giữ nhịp tăng trưởng, vững vàng về itch

Một số dự báo chính của KBSV về kinh tế Việt Nam trong năm 2026:

1) Tăng trưởng GDP năm 2026 được dự báo sẽ đạt 9.0%, với động lực chính 
đến từ định hướng đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư công của Chính phủ, dòng 
vốn FDI duy trì tích cực và hoạt động sản xuất công nghiệp phục hồi theo 
xuất khẩu. Về rủi ro, chúng tôi cho rằng Việt Nam sẽ tiếp tục đối mặt với 
một số yếu tố bất định từ bên ngoài, bao gồm: rủi ro xung đột Mỹ – Iran 
kéo dài hơn dự kiến, xu hướng tăng của mặt bằng lãi suất toàn cầu gây gia 
tăng áp lực tài chính và kéo giảm tổng cầu, cùng nguy cơ vỡ bong bóng AI.

2) Chúng tôi nâng dự báo lạm phát bình quân năm 2026 của Việt Nam lên 
mức 4.7% YoY, cao hơn mục tiêu kiểm soát lạm phát của Chính phủ, dựa 
trên kịch bản dự báo thận trọng hơn về diễn biến xung đột địa chính trị và 
giá năng lượng thế giới trong ngắn hạn.

3) Mặt bằng lãi suất trong quý IV được dự báo sẽ ổn định quanh vùng hiện 
tại và không xuất hiện thêm nhịp tăng đột biến, nhờ dòng tiền cải thiện 
từ giải ngân đầu tư công và kỳ vọng có thêm các biện pháp hỗ trợ thanh 
khoản trung dài hạn từ phía Chính phủ và NHNN.

4) Tỷ giá USD/VND trong quý cuối năm có thể chịu một số sức ép do Fed dự 
báo còn thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm. Dù vậy, chúng tôi cho 
rằng tỷ giá USD/VND khó tăng mạnh mà chỉ quay lại mức đầu năm, dựa 
trên kỳ vọng chỉ số DXY không biến động mạnh, nguồn cung ngoại tệ cải 
thiện (FDI giải ngân, kiều hối, nâng hạng TTCK) và chênh lệch lãi suất 
VND–USD duy trì trạng thái dương.

Tham khảo nội dung chi tiết trong báo cáo tại đây.

### Page 4

21/10/2026

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025. 
— PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ. 
Tham khảo nội dung chi tiết trong báo cáo tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành cây nến xanh thân dài trên khung tuần và đáng chú ý là cho phản ứng 
bật tăng ngay tại cạnh dưới của mẫu hình tam giác đi ngang trung hạn, cho thấy tín hiệu 
tích cực đã quay trở lại với xu hướng ngắn hạn. Trên khung ngày, sau 2 phiên điều chỉnh, 
rung lắc vào đầu tuần, chỉ số cũng đảo chiều tăng điểm khá dốc, với 3 cây nến xanh liên 
tiếp với thanh khoản được tăng cường, củng cố thêm tín hiệu xác nhận cho đà hồi phục. 
Sau nhịp bật tăng mạnh từ đáy, VNINDEX được dự báo sẽ sớm gặp phải áp lực điều chỉnh 
khi quay lên điểm giữa của dải đi ngang theo mẫu hình tam giác, tương ứng vùng kháng cự 
kế tiếp tại quanh 1810. Tuy nhiên, xu hướng ngắn hạn vẫn có phần nghiêng về chiều tăng, 
với tín hiệu xác nhận đáy của một số mã cổ phiếu sau quá trình sụt giảm kéo dài.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1850 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1810 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1750 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1710 (+-15)

## Document 351: First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-10)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-10).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ labour market surveys, September quarter
2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:01 November 04 2026 Share
The unemployment rate rose to 5.7% in the September quarter, against expectations for a
small decline. Spare capacity in the labour market is keeping wage pressures subdued.
Unemployment rate: 5.7% (prev: 5.6%, Westpac: 5.5%, RBNZ: 5.5%, mkt: 5.5%)
Employment change: -0.1% (prev: +0.3%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.3%, mkt: +0.4%)

### Page 2

Participation rate: 70.4% (prev: 70.6%, Westpac: 70.8%, RBNZ: 70.6%, mkt: 70.7%) 
Labour costs (private sector): +0.5% (prev: +0.7%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.4%, mkt: +0.5%)

The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in New Zealand’s labour market. The
unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an almost five-year run of increases.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-
living pressures. 
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise 0.1% fall in employment in the
September quarter, after a downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That went against
the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward
momentum emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure had outpaced the MEI for the
previous nine quarters). It was also softer than suggested by the business-focused Quarterly
Employment Survey (QES), which recorded a 0.2% rise in full-time equivalent employees, a 0.1% rise
in filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. 
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in labour force participation rate, from 70.6%
to 70.4%. These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were indicative of sampling error in the survey. 
This is why we generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, as it’s less vulnerable to
sampling error. And in that respect, the message on the jobs market remains weak: to the extent that
employment may be rising, it’s still not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population (which is
itself a fairly low bar, as net migration remains at a relatively low ebb). 
The HLFS also recorded a sharp 1.6% fall in hours worked (although usual hours worked were down
just 0.4%). This seems to have been influenced by Matariki – a floating holiday that was only
introduced a few years ago, so will not be picked up by the seasonal adjustment. This may have also
been responsible for a marked shift from full-time to part-time employment, as some people may have
been pushed into the part-time category simply by virtue of only working four days during the survey
week. 
The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter, in line with our expectations
overall but a bit weaker in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while there was a
surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector wages. There was a notable jump in the number of
employers citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay rates, although the size of
those increases varied a great deal, with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate. 
Overall, we think the details are soft from the RBNZ’s perspective – an important consideration ahead
of the 9 December Monetary Policy Statement, as there are few other major data releases between
now and then. The unemployment rate is a more reliable signal than the employment measures, and
the ongoing rise in unemployment points to more slack in the labour market than the RBNZ was hoping
to see in an economy that has been growing for over a year now. The doves on the policy committee
will be encouraged in their view that there is limited scope for headline inflation shocks to pass through
into second-round effects. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

## Document 366: GS SALESTRADING_ Taiwan National Stabilization Fund and Tighter Short Sell Restriction _ GSTW

Source PDF: all report pdf/GS SALESTRADING_ Taiwan National Stabilization Fund and Tighter Short Sell Restriction _ GSTW.pdf
Report source: line報告備份

### Page 1

GS SALESTRADING: Taiwan National
Stabilization Fund and Tighter Short Sell
Restriction | GSTW
9 November 2026 | 8:13 AM Taiwan Standard Time
Elaine Kuo
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C. Taipei Branch
FICC & Equities
Robert Wang
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C. Taipei Branch

Historical day last Friday in Taiwan with index down close to 10% before making a 2k

points climb intraday.  Index futures also touched limit down briefly - last time it
happened was during Jul'26 degrossing. Due to such volatility, FSC has decided to
intervene the market. The Desk stayed back on Friday to put out below notes. Special
thanks to our trading desk head Elaine Kuo, sales Jill Huang, and compliance Daniel
Lin.
Taiwan’s NATIONAL STABILIZATION FUND held a meeting in the afternoon
[2026/11/6] and agreed to activate the National Stabilization Fund (NSF) with

NT$500b or US$15.2b usable capital to stabilize the mkt. NSF had a good track
record in its previous 9 interventions. The mkt rallied 6% on avg in the following week
after announcement, and +22% over one year. However, 6 out of 9 times experienced
additional 5%~28% drawdowns within 6 months after activation.
While National Stabilization Fund announcement provides a sentiment boost to the
stock market, the actual deployed capital could be limited - only NT$0.76b~122.7b

(US$23m~3.7b) were actually deployed in the past 9 instances vs. NSF NT$500b
(US$15.2b) usable capital. That being said, from previous experience, NSF tends to
buy large cap tech/ financials with heavy index weighting (see below).

Furthermore, Taiwan's Financial Supervisory Commission (FSC) announced five
market stabilization measures effective November 9, 2026, without specifying an
end date, to calm stock market volatility following sharp selloffs in the Taiwan Market:
1. Intraday Short-Selling Cap: Lowered the daily short sell limit from 30% to 3% of
30-day average daily trading volume (exemptions apply to hedging and market-
making activities).
2. Margin Requirement Hike: Increased the minimum margin requirement for

retails’ margin short selling listed stocks (融券保證⾦成數) from 90% to 130%.
11/9/26, 8:28 AM GS SALESTRADING: Taiwan National Stabilization Fund and Tighter Short Sell Restriction | GSTW
https://marquee.gs.com/s/content/markets/18f8509b-7aa3-4cdb-8093-13ae5c123cb2 2/9

### Page 3

3. Expanded Collateral Scope: Allowed investors to use alternative liquid collateral
(such as foreign securities held via sub-brokerage accounts) to meet margin calls
and cover financing shortfalls.
4. Encourage Share Buybacks: Urged listed companies to initiate treasury share
buybacks based on their financial and capital conditions.
5. Encourage Investor Communications: Encouraged listed companies to host
institutional investor conferences and earnings presentations to reinforce

corporate fundamentals.
These measures are very similar to last time in 2025 April. Some investors concern
that lowered daily short sell limit (for institutional investors’ short selling) and higher
margin requirement (for retail investors’ margin short selling) will hurt market
participation and liquidity.

realize each day a loss or a gain which is a multiple (for leveraged ETFs and ETNs) or the inverse (for inverse ETFs and ETNs) of the

performance on that day of their underlying index or benchmark. For this reason, if they are held for a period longer than one day, their
performance over such longer periods of time can differ significantly from the stated multiple of the performance (or the inverse of the
performance) of their underlying index or benchmark during the same period of time. This effect can be magnified in volatile markets.
Prior to entering into a transaction in leveraged or inverse ETFs, you should be aware of the general risks associated with such

transactions. You should not enter into leveraged or inverse ETFs transactions unless you understand the nature and extent of your risk
exposure. You should also be satisfied that the leveraged or inverse ETFs transaction is appropriate for you in light of your circumstances
and financial condition. ETFs and ETNs that are linked to commodity futures do not offer direct exposure to the commodity's spot price,
and may perform differently than the spot price for the commodity itself. Performance differential can be magnified if a specific

condition persists in the market for a commodity that creates a disparity between near-term future prices and long-term future prices
and may lead to unexpected performance results. Other factors, such as roll yield, transaction costs, management fees, and taxes may
cause deviation in performance between the spot price of a commodity and commodity futures. You should not assume that an ETF or
ETN that is linked to commodity futures will provide an effective hedge because of a negative correlation with equities or other asset

swaps, and other derivatives as well as non-investment-grade securities - give rise to substantial risk and are not available to nor suitable
for all investors. If you have any questions about whether you are eligible to enter into these transactions with Goldman Sachs, please

values of which are influenced by foreign currencies, effectively assume currency risk.

## Document 379: LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI  investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

LatAm’s silicon dividend: 
tailwinds from the AI  
investment boom in the U.S.

1 
Authors 
Fancisco Campos 
Chief LatAm Economist

Beatriz Garcia Nunes 
LatAm Economist

October 28, 2026 
Key messages: 
- U.S. investment has broken away from Europe and China since 2023, 
powered by AI-related equipment, software and research spending. 
- The AI CapEx boom demands commodities and merchandise that LatAm 
can supply.  
- Latin America’s export gains are real but concentrated. Copper, computer 
and electronics products provide the clearest links to the U.S. AI buildout. 
- Chile is the region’s most direct copper beneficiary. Mexico captures the 
windfall stemming from increases hardware demand and trade-diversion. In 
these cases, the AI-related tailwinds have undergirded appreciable 
improvements in external accounts. 
- The cases for nickel and zinc illustrate that the dividend is not widespread. 
U.S. imports of the former have mostly returned toward pre-pandemic levels, 
while for the latter bilateral gains partly reflect supplier substitution. 
- While expanding AI use, supply shortages and incentives can extend the 
CapEx boom, its financial viability poses downside risks. 
Another ‘American exceptionalism’ story  
Investment has become one of the sharpest dividing lines among the world’s 
core economic blocs. Since 2024, U.S. gross fixed capital formation has firmed 
up appreciably. Meanwhile, investment in Europe has broadly stalled while in 
China’s it has retreated, the drivers of which are beyond the scope of this report 
(Figure 1).  
The U.S. divergence has largely been an AI story: a burst of spending on 
computing power, digital infrastructure and intellectual property has offset 
weakness in conventional construction and housing. Indeed, total U.S. fixed 
investment is nearly 12% above its 2023 level. Within it, investment in 
information-processing equipment has risen by roughly 57%, software outlays by 
about 35%, and research and development by around 15%. By contrast, non-
residential structures and residential investment sit below their 2023 levels 
(Figure 2).

### Page 2

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 2

Figure 1: The global investment divergence

Figure 2: AI-related categories lead U.S. investment

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Eurostat, National Bureau of Statistics of 
China, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Latin America’s commodity channel 
AI is not merely a semiconductor story. Chips require specialized minerals; data 
centers require electricity and cooling; electricity requires grids, transformers and 
storage. That chain pulls in copper, aluminum, silver, tin, lithium and selected 
semiconductor inputs, along with natural gas and uranium. Latin America has 
meaningful exposure to several of these inputs, but copper has emerged as the 
commodity with the cleanest and most economically significant regional link. 
Copper is used throughout the AI infrastructure stack, including power cables, 
substations, transformers, server boards, heat sinks and cooling systems. Rising 
U.S. copper demand has lifted imports from Chile and Peru, particularly the 
former (Figure 3). T he effect matters beyond merchandise export volumes. 
Favorable terms-of-trade have helped Chile generate a current -account surplus 
for the first time in roughly fifteen years (0.2% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4 -
quarter rolling basis ) and have allowed Peru to log the largest surplus in about 
forty-five years (4.5% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis).

28 October 2026

### Page 3

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 3

Figure 3: Copper epitomized the commodity link

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Not every metal is copper 
The broader mining story demands more caution, though. U.S. nickel imports have 
recovered toward pre-Covid levels, but the upswing has not displayed the same 
persistence as copper. Mexico has captured a disproportionate share of the 
bilateral improvement, yet that gain looks more like normalization than a 
sustained AI -driven break (Figure 4). Zinc tells a similar story. U.S. real imports 
stand shy of their pre-pandemic levels, and stronger purchases from Mexico and 
Peru partly reflect a reallocation away from Brazil rather than a decisive increase 
in U.S. demand (Figure 5).

Figure 4: Nickel: recovery, not a boom

Figure 5: Zinc: a substitution story

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

28 October 2026

### Page 4

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 4

Mexico’s hardware advantage 
The larger non-commodity prize is in computers and electronics. U.S. imports of 
these products have sky -rocketed, while the supplier mix has shifted away from 
China. Mexico and various Asian economies have gained share on the back of this 
double-whammy impulse (Figure 6). 
This helps explain why Mexico’s non-automotive manufacturing exports, notably 
electronics, have outpaced automotive shipments to the U.S. and pressures from 
higher oil prices. The resulting non -oil trade surplus has offset pressure from the 
oil balance and helped Mexico generate a balanced current account (0% of GDP 
as of Q2 2026 on a 4 -quarter rolling basis). The external boost is meaningful, 
although it sho uld not necessarily be interpreted as bona fide  evidence of 
nearshoring, in our view.

Figure 6: U.S. computer & electronics imports going into overdrive

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research.

How much runway remains? 
Quantifying or mapping out a timeframe for the AI investment outlook is beyond 
the scope of this piece or our expertise. Nevertheless, we’d venture to argue that 
the capex cycle appears more likely to mature gradually than to stop abruptly . 
One reason is that the investment case is shifting from frontier -model training 
toward enterprise inference . Capacity that once served development can 
increasingly run commercial workloads for companies. That creates a path to 
revenue even if artificial general intelligence (AGI) crystallizes later than 
expected. It also spreads demand to a much larger corporate customer base.  
A second reason is scarcity. Graphics processing units (GPUs) remain tight, while 
power availability, grid connections, cooling equipment, construction capacity 
and data -center complexity limit how quickly supply can respond. Bottlenecks 
may therefore exte nd the cycle. The constraint is especially relevant for copper 
28 October 2026

### Page 5

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 5

and power infrastructure, where projects have long lead times and permitting 
hurdles. 
A third reason is strategic. AI development resembles a prisoner’s dilemma at 
both the corporate and national levels. Each participant may worry about costs, 
safety and control, yet slowing unilaterally risks surrendering technological, 
commercial or natio nal security advantages. The same tension is visible in calls 
for guardrails alongside continued spending. Competition can therefore sustain 
capex even when short-term returns are uncertain. 
We acknowledge the risks, nonetheless. Higher borrowing costs raise the hurdle 
rate for an infrastructure buildout financed partly through debt. More important, 
revenues must eventually justify the installed capital base. The bearish case is not 
that AI la cks value; it is that infrastructure spending may run ahead of 
monetization, compromising some companies’ repayment capacity. Public 
opposition to the development of AI infrastructure and/or adoption of AI 
technologies could also slow down the technology’s roll-out. 
The regional balance  
Summing up, the AI investment boom in the U .S. is delivering a dividend to parts 
of Latin America, not the region as a whole. Chile and Peru benefit through copper 
prices and export volumes. Mexico benefits through electronics manufacturing 
and the replacement of Chinese supply. For Brazil and Colombia, the AI dividend 
is rather muted. Where materialized, furthermore, the dividend supports trade 
and current accounts more clearly rather than improving the domestic 
investment. 
These dynamics should persist while U.S. AI investment remains supply -
constrained and strategically protected. If widespread adoption advances and 
the strategic race intensifies, the impulse can last. If financing costs rise further 
or monetization disappo ints, the cycle may narrow. Either way, copper and 
Mexican electronics remain the region’s most visible gauges of the U.S. AI 
investment appetite.

28 October 2026

### Page 6

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
LatAm’s silicon dividend: tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. – October 28, 2026
